Cursor✓
Tamarind Bay won because his OR 128 mark, 24f stamina (16 runs in the last 20), and Good-ground jumping record (G 1-6) fitted this Bangor Class 2 handicap chase exactly. Fitness indicators supported the run — rtf 57% and a sliding mark (3.6→2.8) — and he justified strong market favouritism at SP 1.2 by beating rivals stepping up from weaker recent class.
Right The board pick Tamarind Bay at 1.16 correctly flagged the horse whose rating, trip, ground and form profile best matched a 24f Good Class 2 chase.
Improve class: When a top-rated favourite has proven form at the race class and trip, cap win_prob on rivals whose recent wins sit at C3/C4 unless their ratings and course-and-distance evidence clearly close the gap.
NVIDIA✓
Tamarind Bay neutralised Sure Touch early, producing a strong turn of foot to clear the last and score at 1.2 starting price on good ground at Bangor-on-Dee. The winner’s pace was economical without being extreme, allowing them to hold off the market-leader Sure Touch in the closing stages.
Right Sure Touch was fancied with a 0.52 win probability, but Tamarind Bay’s victory confirms the market assessment was fair, if not overly optimistic on the favourite.
Improve market: adjust staking expectations when a horse wins at 1.2SP despite a strong prior selection.
Lucy✓
Tamarind Bay demonstrated a strong performance with a dominant win at the Goffs Yorton Sale 10th September Handicap Steeple Chase, benefiting from a favorable ground condition. The horse maintained its speed well throughout the race and managed to pull away from the competition in the final stretch.
Right We correctly identified Tamarind Bay as a strong contender with a win probability of 0.45.
Improve form: Keep an eye on recent consistent form for future races to ensure optimal performance.

AI post mortem
AI Post Mortem Promptcopy brief
Cursor✗
VERDICT: 0.17 on Hickory was fair in hindsight—Hickory won at SP 11.0, so that estimate was not too high and may have been slightly low. Classic lost as the 6.6 market leader carrying OR 100 top weight (9-10) with no Ascot course form (0-3, CD 0-2) in a Class 2 8f handicap on Good ground. At 35 days since last run he looked less race-ready than Hickory (LR21), and the Hannon yard also ran Witch Hunter—same-yard dynamics and Hickory’s prominent pace role (p) with proven course form (3-12, CD 1-2) from draw 11 suited this race better than Classic’s profile from draw 6.
Missed We elevated Classic on the 15.0→6.6 market move despite zero course wins and ignored that the winning profile was Hickory’s course/draw/pace fit, not the favourite’s.
Improve course: require a positive course or C-D record before backing short-priced runners at Ascot Class handicaps, especially when the same trainer fields a second runner that may affect pace tactics.
Groq✗
The 0.22 win probability was too high in hindsight. Classic was hit by a very strong early pace that forced him to sit off the pace and he never got a clear run. Combined with good ground that exposed his limited stamina after a 10th‑place run, he lacked the finishing speed to stay in the places.
Missed We missed the impact of the fast early pace that left Classic trapped and unable to finish strongly.
Improve pace: target races with a slower early tempo or position him nearer the lead to avoid being blocked.
NVIDIA✗
Classic was pushed forward early by jockey Jim Crowley and tracked the leaders into the final furlong, but struggled to find a clear run on the wide draw and was eased down inside the last 100 metres. Hickory stayed on well under pressure on the rail to draw clear, while Classic never looked likely to land a blow from the wide ten stall.
Missed Classic's wide draw in a testing good-to-firm surface prevented effective rail running and cost valuable ground on the bend.
Improve draw: a tighter assignment would allow Classic to secure a trailing position and preserve ground on the bend.
Lucy✗
Despite inconsistent form, Hickory surprised with a strong performance on the day, taking advantage of an unexpectedly longer price. The horse may have benefited from a tactical ride or better pace to secure the win.
Missed We underestimated Hickory's ability to perform well at longer odds, and we missed the opportunity to consider the potential impact of a better-coordinated riding strategy or more favorable pace.
Improve pace: Analyzing the likely speed profile of each runner could provide valuable insights for betting strategies
⚠ Consensus 3/6 · Hickory

The step to eight furlongs and soft ground are questions, but her close-up six-furlong run suggests she should stay this far better than the others have yet proven.

Highland Girl is the main danger — draw four suits this track, Johnston/Crouch are the strongest trainer-jockey combo on recent numbers, and she is the freshest runner — but she would need to improve sharply on that Goodwood form to overturn Girl Scout’s superiority on what we have seen so far.

Official Ratingspedigree and breeze-up/trial evidenceearly pace map and run-style match-up
Impasto gets the nod mainly on connections in a field with almost no exposed form. Andrew Balding sits #2 on the six-month trainer table and sends plenty of ready debutants, while Colin Keane (#8 among jockeys) is a positive booking. Draw six sits in the neutral middle third at Ascot 7f — not as helpful as the high-bias stalls — but it avoids the clear low-draw penalty that hurts Korsakov (1) and Senor Blues (2). With ten unraced horses, trainer/jockey strike rate and a fair draw are about as much signal as exists; Impasto has the strongest combination of those.

The main risk is uncertainty, not a single obvious rival. Shimmering Shadow (draw 9, William Buick) and Ayuq (draw 8, Gosden) combine high-draw advantage with top-tier stables, and Chymbulak is the only runner with a racecourse reference — a Class 3 Kempton second over 7f, albeit on AW and 5.5L behind the winner. Too Darn Regal has the best draw (11) but weaker yard/jockey form. Without ORs, pedigrees, or a reliable pace picture, Impasto’s true chance is only modestly above the ~9% base rate for an 11-runner field; this is a thin edge, not a strong conviction bet.

CursorSplashing Out · 18%
Risk Galway 18/20 between wins flags jumping or stamina fragility despite the Wexford success.
OR marks for Cocofred and SpeisialtaKilbeggan course form for Splashing Outearly pace and whether blinkers force a prominent trip
Splashing Out heads my estimates on a clear class edge (OR 113, best-known figure bar the out-of-form My Trump Card) and last-start credibility: he won at Wexford on Good over 17f, matching today’s ground and near-trip, with an earlier Tramore 16f Good win showing the trip is within scope. Blinkers and a 22-day refresh are positives in a field where several rivals are either very lightly raced or returning from long gaps.

The main counter is Eye Of A Tiger: he is the only runner with Kilbeggan course-and-distance form (1-2), carries a useful 10-9 off OR 105, and arrives off a Ballinrobe win with J J Slevin booked. I still prefer Splashing Out because the Galway 18/20 is a serious completion red flag for Eye Of A Tiger, and his latest run was another poor Galway finish (15/20), which tempers the local track angle. Cocofred (De Bromhead/O’Keeffe, Roscommon 16f Good winner) is the main danger if his unknown mark leaves him leniently treated.

CursorPinot Gris · 14%
Risk Top weight in a deep staying handicap where Kilbeggan course specialists carry less.
handicap peak vs current OR 138jumping completion under chase pressure over 20.5fearly pace/scenario over this trip
Pinot Gris is the most credible winner on form independent of the 1.1 placeholders: he is the highest-rated stayer here (OR 138), has completed cleanly in recent chases, and his Galway second over 22f on yielding was the best piece of collective form in the field — on that day he beat Charlie Luciano, Soldante, and Prince Palace while only finishing 6L behind the winner in a 20-runner marathon. The 20.5f good-ground trip fits a horse built for stamina rather than sharp 16f winners, and De Bromhead/O’Keeffe are strong NH connections even with quiet T14/J14 figures.

The main doubt is handicap compression: 12st is a lot in a 13-runner handicap full of course winners and lightweights. Charlie Luciano (won over 26f at Kilbeggan in July on good), Grange Walk (3rd over 20f at Ballinrobe before a brought-down run here), and Hashtag Pretender (won latest but fell at Punchestown and won over 16f) all have angles to overturn the favourite if jumping or trip fitness breaks wrong for the top weight.
